- (de) madam president, we cannot - we have no right - to resign ourselves to hearing more and more frequently about accidents which cause serious damage on roads, railways and waterways, also because the people involved do not take the transport of dangerous goods seriously enough or because, through ignorance or lack of training of the driver or the person responsible for the various means of transport, it happens too often that a benign accident turns into a catastrophe. as an austrian - and i think we all do - i have a vivid memory of the catastrophe which last year took many lives in the tauern tunnel and which subsequently imposed for many months the task of rebuilding the destruction caused by that fire, a task whose financial impact was enormous. the renovation, which took several months, cut off this important north-south route. the resulting diversionary trafficking has pushed thousands of eu citizens to the limits of the bearable. in some communities in my country, it was hell. our response to such disasters must be prevention and the draft directive before us creates an important basis for having well trained safety officials and at the same time doing what needs to be done. we should not simply be moving european law towards greater security. we must also ensure that the member states respect the deadlines for implementing our projects and, more importantly, that these projects are subsequently implemented. i would ask you not to allow this to become another area in which we will have to lament the lack of implementation. i would like to make one final point: we cannot simply fill in one of the gaps in the safety net and ignore the fact that there is still a lot to be done in the field of transport safety. in this connection, i would ask the commissioner responsible, who is here, to submit as soon as possible a text which will increase safety in tunnels in the future so that we in europe will not have to deal with disasters of this magnitude again.

- (de) mr president, our colleague mr van der laan recommends that we approve the discharge for the 1997 financial year and, as previous speakers have said, we interpret this proposal as a gesture of goodwill towards the new commission. discharge would be based on the assumption that the commission implements a broad reform programme, as specified in paragraph 1 of the resolution. however, those who believe, after the rejection of the discharge for 1996, that the granting of the discharge for 1997 will put things right are mistaken. i think there is one important question that remains unanswered: to what extent is the new commission actually prepared to submit itself and its officials to the scrutiny of parliament and the court of justice ? to give you an example, when the committee on budgetary control was reconstituted after the elections, one of the first issues it faced was the fléchard case, which concerned fraud in connection with the export of butter to the former ussr in the early 1990s. on 7 january 1994, senior officials decided to lift the fine of almost €18 million imposed on the company in question. this decision was in clear breach of the community provisions in force. we only learned about it at the end of 1998 through an anonymous letter. all i have heard so far from the members of the prodi commission on this case is the message: let us stop investigating old cases over and over again and instead try to look to the future. the most monstrous thing about this case is that a director-general and the directors concerned at the time innocently stated that the minutes of the decisive meeting of 7 january 1994 had unfortunately disappeared inexplicably. there was not one copy of the minutes, but several. each participant had received a personal copy, but today, unfortunately, they all disappeared, all of them! we cannot say: let's pass the baton! this is a case which should be added to the list of internal cases examined by olaf, but to my knowledge this case has never been reported to olaf. this is also a matter which should be brought to the attention of the competent national judicial authorities, because, in the final analysis, the disappearance of documents and minutes is not a sin but a punishable act, according to article 241 of the belgian penal code. i will therefore simply say that we will certainly discuss this again in the discharge procedure for 1998.
- (de) mr president, if the santer commission has failed, it is also because it has suffered a bitter failure in terms of financial control. the future of the new commission will therefore depend largely on the speed with which reforms are undertaken and financial control is restored. in this connection, it has already been repeated on several occasions that the commission intends to improve and considerably strengthen its ex post controls and that these should be carried out in full independence, without any future hiding of irregularities under the carpet. we can of course only welcome these initiatives. what i do not understand is that the price to be paid for these measures would be the disappearance of a priori controls, carried out in complete independence - let me stress the term independence. until now, the commission could only make payments if the authorising officer had signed the order and the financial controller had stamped his agreement on the visa. it is therefore a two-pronged approach. in the future, one key will be enough. the financial controller will no longer be able to carry out a priori checks. at least this is what the reform plans, as they are currently being discussed in the commission, suggest. with all due respect, mr kinnock, it is a bit like deciding to dismantle our police services because they are not able to prevent crime. what should be the point here is to make controls more efficient. this should be achieved by no longer requiring financial controllers to systematically target every payment transaction. because the one who has to control everything ends up controlling nothing. a priori checks should therefore in future only be carried out in cases where uncertainties and risks exist. officials in charge of financial control should exercise their functions in a decentralised manner, i.e. in the directorates-general, with colleagues who spend the money. this would make it easier to reach them when problems arise. this would therefore make checks less time-consuming and burdensome. but financial controllers must work in complete independence. this is the fundamental difference from what the commission envisages when it talks about decentralisation. officials in charge of financial control should not even be subordinate to the directors-general, as the commission seems to be considering. this is what we learn from the leonardo case, where the internal auditors had alerted the relevant management, which had not thought it appropriate to act on their warning. only independence can therefore guarantee the effectiveness of controls. this is the position of a significant majority of the members of the committee on budgetary control. as regards ex post controls, the commission has stated that it is in favour of this principle of independence. why should the same not apply to a priori checks? i think we should stress this point in the vote tomorrow. mrs theato has already mentioned it. on that point, i can only support her.

- (de) madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to say a few words about the manner in which this oral question was tabled and the ideological tactics which led to it. i also know that in addition to the economic and financial experts, many social democrats in this house share our criticism. we know the problem. this is the instability of the markets as a result of speculation. but we must tell the authors of this question that - as the commissioner has just pointed out to us in such an impressive way - all instability does not stem from speculation. the tobin tax could not have avoided the ems crises of 1992 and 1993 or the crisis of the south-east asian currencies in 1997. the authors of this question do not propose any solution but call for a new tax, call for more bureaucracy, a measure against the market mechanisms and seize the plenary with an intergroup approach bypassing the competent parliamentary committee, in contradiction with several decisions of the parliament. in my view, and i say this clearly, this is an ideological question and not a solution-oriented one. i repeat that i consider this to be the wrong way forward. why do we reject the tobin tax and consider it so highly liable to bail? firstly, as the commissioner has also confirmed, because it represents a very heavy burden on the capital markets. a reduced tax would also have a serious impact on the capital markets, as such a tax on speculation would severely reduce the profitability of investments. moreover, such a measure would also severely hamper the development of financial markets. this would be contrary to the principle of free movement of capital which is at the heart of the internal market. secondly, speculative capital is difficult to identify. thirdly, tax measures can easily be circumvented if they are not introduced simultaneously in all countries because there are many tax havens. fourthly, the measure is not technically feasible, it is very bureaucratic and administrative and it is difficult to control. the measure we should take is a part of the resolution that is presented to us. this is about strengthening banking supervision. the aim is to clarify the responsibility for transactions. it is a regulatory framework at international level. the aim is to examine national laws to determine whether they correspond to the rules of the basel committee and whether the latter can be disseminated more widely. i have a big problem with a crucial point in this resolution which we cannot accept as it stands. this is point 9. if we have followed the commissioner correctly, we know that the commission's knowledge of this matter will lead it to say no to the tobin tax. if the knowledge, debate and arguments are as we put them and as i believe they are justified, i think it is irresponsible to mobilize the commission for another six months and thereby create uncertainty on the financial and capital markets. the capital market does not expect us to bring uncertainty, it needs clarity. that is why we reject paragraph 9 and call for a separate vote.

- the joint debate on the following motions for resolutions on côte d'ivoire: - b5-0063/2000 by mr van den bos, on behalf of the eldr group, on the situation in côte d'ivoire; - b5-0068/2000 by mr sjöstedt and mr alavanos, on behalf of the gue/ngl group, on the situation in côte d'ivoire; - b5-0077/2000 by mrs rod and others, on behalf of the green group in the european parliament, on côte d'ivoire; - b5-0087/2000 by mr novelli and others, on behalf of the group of the european people's party, on the coup d'état in côte d'ivoire.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european union was already following with particular attention developments in côte d'ivoire in view of the arrest and imprisonment of opposition leaders, the arrest warrant issued against mr ouattara, the development of inter-ethnic tensions and the deterioration of the economic situation. today our first objective is to encourage a rapid return to the rule of law and the normal functioning of democratic institutions. to this end, the commission decided to initiate the consultation procedure provided for in article 366a of the lomé convention. this procedure is the same as that applied last year in respect of niger and guinea-bissau. the council approved this proposal. the commission and the council invited côte d'ivoire. the ivorian authorities shall have 15 days to respond to this invitation. the consultations must then be concluded within one month. the european parliament shall be kept informed of the progress of these consultations. the objectives of these consultations will be: to make a detailed assessment of the situation in côte d'ivoire; to underline the importance which the european union attaches to respect for the essential elements of article 5 of the lomé convention, namely respect for the rule of law, democracy and human rights; to obtain detailed information on côte d'ivoire's intentions to ensure respect for these essential elements; to reach agreement on the measures to be taken to remedy breaches of these essential elements. the commission and the council are preparing these consultations, but i can already tell you that our aim is to obtain a firm commitment from the ivorian authorities to a return to constitutional order and the announcement of a timetable for this transition. however, if the results of the consultations are not satisfactory, the european union has the possibility at that time to take appropriate measures, including, if necessary, the suspension of all or part of cooperation with that country. so far, the deletion has not been proposed, because we want to avoid penalising the populations. however, for the time being no new financing decisions will be approved except for humanitarian aid projects for the most deprived populations. in conclusion, i can assure you that the commission attaches the greatest importance to the consultations which will be opened in the coming days with côte d'ivoire. it will continue to monitor closely the development of the situation in that country and the respect for human rights, and the european parliament will be kept informed.
- this debate is over. the vote will take place at 5.30 p.m.

- (de) mr president, commissioner, we are continuing today the debate on a balanced strategy for the internal market and consumer protection, starting with an oral question which deals in particular with the contradiction between the principle of the country of origin in the internal market and the principle of the country of destination in consumer protection. such abbreviations are inevitable for oral questions. however, i believe - and the debate has shown this so far - that the discussion of these two principles does not really advance us in this area, especially when it comes to finding an adequate legal framework for e-commerce. there is a legitimate fear - i admit it - that the possibility for consumers to bring a complaint before the courts of their country of residence may slow down or even eliminate the development of electronic commerce. but at the same time we know that this possibility of appeal to national courts is rarely exploited in practice. but we can still less demand that consumers go to the courts of the supplier's country of origin and that suppliers accordingly establish themselves in the member states where consumer protection is least developed and consumer rights are most limited. this would certainly not increase consumer confidence, which e-commerce needs to grow. i therefore believe that neither the new article 15 of this regulation alone nor its limitation to offline trade will really help us. what we lack are systems for extrajudicial settlement of disputes and rapid procedures for settling the so-called small claims. i therefore explicitly welcome the measures proposed by the commission in its communication on the future strategy, in particular the support for initiatives in the sense of online out-of-court dispute resolution procedures and in the sense of a new green paper on access to justice and redress for consumers. i think, however, that it should also be possible to develop with european banks and credit card providers secure electronic money trading and transfer systems that allow for electronic transactions on a per-coup basis. similarly, closer cooperation between consumer protection associations and cross-border competence of these associations could ultimately be more useful to us on a daily basis than a theoretical opposition between the principle of the country of destination and the principle of the country of origin. after the commission's proposals for the new draft regulation have been criticised in this house, i would like to emphasize explicitly that i think the commission's approach is correct. article 13 of the convention already clarifies the jurisdiction of the consumer and most legal experts already believe that this also applies to online commerce. the commission therefore makes no change to the current legal situation. as everyone knows, under article 95 (3) the commission must opt for a high level of consumer protection in all its proposals. i therefore believe that the commission has done the right thing with its proposal. as a socialist group we also support the proposals of our colleague mr wallis from the committee on legal affairs and internal market, who has proposed some changes to the draft regulation but also wants us to complete the current system with new procedures. in conclusion, i would like to say on behalf of my group that we accept a change to the country of origin principle, provided that additional procedures strengthen the legal protection of consumers by offering them new possibilities for legal protection.

the rapporteur. - (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i must apologize for my already rather scratchy voice, but as an austrian member of parliament i have already had to explain and discuss a great deal today. i should like to begin by thanking the commission for its initiative and for the two draft directives we are debating today. in doing so, it fills two serious gaps in the internal market which are of great importance to the european economy and to the five million third-country nationals established in the european union as workers or self-employed. it should be remembered that the present situation is such that, as far as workers are concerned, we have judgments of the european court of justice; in particular, the judgments in the rush portuguesa and van der elst cases. the court held that the freedom to provide services must make it possible to second thirdcountry nationals who are employees under a service contract without requiring them to have a work permit. the issue of the conditions for granting visas and residence permits was not, however, explicitly addressed by the european court of justice, nor was it subsequently clarified by the member states. as regards the abolition of work permits, not all member states have been able to follow the court of justice's conclusions and still maintain a series of intolerable barriers to crossborder service provision, which are often particularly insurmountable for small and mediumsized enterprises. for the selfemployed, the current community legal acquis does not provide for the right of third-country nationals to provide crossborder services. in any case, legislation is therefore necessary in this area. the two draft directives are designed to facilitate the freedom to provide services for eu businesses. there is therefore no question of intrinsic rights for third-country nationals who rely on freedom of movement. all regulations concerning entry and residence must be considered ancillary to this freedom to provide services. i therefore agree with the committee on legal affairs and the internal market of this parliament that the legal bases chosen by the commission are correct and that the contrary view expressed in the expert opinion of the council's legal service is incorrect. i therefore see no real legal obstacle to the rapid pursuit of the two draft directives in the council. i also think that, compared with the commission's proposal, the amendments proposed by parliament should make it easier for the council to adopt directives. many of our amendments are based on reservations which have also been expressed in the council, and we are trying to reconcile these reservations with what the law in force and common economic sense require of us. as regards the council's approval, i can only call on the commission to adopt as fully as possible the amendments adopted by parliament, even if some of them deviate significantly from the commission's original proposals. i am convinced that we can more easily obtain the council's agreement on this basis. let us now turn to the main amendments we are proposing. a key difference is that instead of a 'service card plus proof of each order' system, we no longer have the possibility of requiring the host member state to make a prior declaration for each order. we do not think this system is practical. to compensate for this, however, it is necessary to remove all possible obstacles to the issuing of the service card and, in relation to the commission's proposal, to strengthen the conditions for issuing the service card. the requirements of regular employment, legal residence and insurance cover must be met not only at the time of issuing the card but also for the period covering the entire period of its validity and the three months thereafter. the host state would thus have the guarantee that the worker or selfemployed person will return to his country of residence at the end of the benefit and that he is covered by insurance in the event of illness or accident. similarly, the worker's situation as regards entry and residence should be clarified by means of an adversarial procedure prior to the issuing of a service card. we envisage that the eu service card should not only be applied for in all member states but could also be applied for in particular member states. i think that this system is also more suited to practical needs. i also hope that the proposal will reduce the minimum duration of the work concerned to three months and will make the validity of the service card more flexible in relation to the duration of the work. we are, however, of the opinion that the maximum validity of the service card should be fixed at 12 months. as regards the selfemployed, we are also proposing, in addition to the amendments already mentioned, to strengthen the residence criterion and to provide for the possibility of combating potential abuses resulting from the recruitment of fake selfemployed workers. i would like to briefly look at the amendments which go beyond those tabled by the committee on legal affairs and the internal market. i would like to say, of course, that i support the amendments which have been unanimously adopted by the legal affairs committee. i myself tabled four amendments on behalf of my group, which essentially concern the way in which a council decision should be cited. i must say that i have received the most contradictory information from the services of parliament as to how to cite this decision: only the number, only the date, both, or the detailed citation. two amendments were rejected by the services on the grounds that their content had already been taken into account in the report. i withdraw the other two amendments - amendment no 18 to the report on the selfemployed and amendment no 21 to the report on employed persons. i can only call on the conference of presidents to reach agreement as soon as possible on the correct comitology procedure for subpoenas. this would greatly facilitate the work of the rapporteurs in the future. a proposal for an amendment was also tabled to the two reports by mr de palacio. i regret to say that i cannot support this proposal because it touches on the substance of the result which we reached unanimously in the committee on legal affairs and internal market and i would like to stick to that result which has been the result of lengthy discussions. in conclusion, i would like to thank all the members who have supported me in the legal affairs committee in the preparation of these reports, which were not very easy. i would like to mention in particular mr wieland, who played the ungrateful role of ghost rapporteur and who took a much more important part in the preparation of this report than is customary, bringing excellent and constructive ideas, without being able to take the laurels of the rapporteur. that is why i wanted to emphasize this point in this chamber.
- (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should first like to thank the rapporteur and mr wieland very warmly for the efforts they have made to bring together the various amendments and the various interests in this report in such a homogeneous way. these two proposals make a significant contribution to the implementation of one of the four essential principles of the internal market, the freedom to provide services. there is no doubt that the new regulation on crossborder services will improve the functioning of the internal market and the competitiveness and efficiency of businesses. the strict conditions attached to the establishment of the eu service card - and i support this point from mr wieland - are essential because they help to prevent crimes such as illegal emigration and fictitious contracts. why do i think this directive is so essential? the three reasons are: the economic importance of third country labour in the eu, the competitiveness of firms and the smooth functioning of the internal market. i therefore welcome the establishment of a strict framework and i ask that the controls carried out by the member states be organised in the most efficient and simple way. in conclusion, i would like to ask the commission the following question, because i am regularly asked about this: will the directive be detrimental to the accession negotiations? what is your interpretation of article 1, given that it plays an important role for our country, which is on the border of many new states, and in the accession negotiations?

the rapporteur. - (de) madam president, ladies and gentlemen, my equal report is in the form of an opinion in the context of the consultation process for a new community initiative. the aim of this initiative is transnational collaboration to promote new methods of combating discrimination and inequalities of all kinds in the labour market. this report was very costly, since it also includes the opinions of four additional committees, namely the industry, regional policy, women's rights and home affairs committees. this community initiative succeeds the adapt and employment pioneering programmes, but has a much smaller budget of around €2.8 billion. it also takes a completely new approach to developing innovative employment models on a geographical or sectoral basis through transnational development partnerships. the intention is clear: to develop transnational projects, projects which focus on employment guidelines, namely employability, entrepreneurship, adaptability and equal opportunities for men and women. the objective pursued is indeed to be welcomed. it also has a broad consensus in the assembly. equal must also look into national employment action plans, i.e. whether these action plans allow for their implementation. this is an important community initiative which, like the urban, leader and interreg initiatives, is financed by the structural funds. in my report i tried to reduce excessive administrative expenditure and to make the development partnership framework a little more open and flexible. i think that technical assistance is necessary. however, because of the problems we have had with the last bats, no new bats should be created before parliament's resolution and in this way the political responsibility should not be transferred to any technical assistance body. however, technical assistance is still needed. particular attention will also be needed to dissemination of results and mutual learning through best practice and mainstreaming. i have endeavoured to ensure that this report is adopted as widely as possible. this has led to a reduction in the number of amendments - from 100 in committee to 22 in plenary - which has created many opportunities for compromise. we have even managed to reach a compromise on the asylumseekers' question. i would ask the president to vote on the equal report on wednesday instead of tuesday so that we can work on some of the details. it seems to me important that this community initiative can start on time and that this report has broad support in this house, so that the commission is also obliged to take parliament's constructive proposals into consideration. the resources earmarked for equal have been set aside by the european parliament, so that the expectations of parliamentarians can also be reflected in the community initiative. in this respect, the european parliament's insistence that amendments to the main points can only be made after a further hearing of the assembly is also justified. equal must live up to its name, that is my firm intention. it must guarantee equal opportunities for all disadvantaged people in the european union. he must prevent the disintegration of our society. it must prevent marginalization from becoming part of everyday life. it must offer everyone the opportunity to benefit from a joint initiative, regardless of age, gender and origin. that is my intention and i ask this house to give the widest possible support to this report.

rural areas cover 80% of the eu's area, but only 25% of the eu's population live there. as a farmer and member of parliament, i have always tried to keep agriculture and the countryside together. i attach particular importance to an integrated approach to rural development. i am convinced that only by networking all the professional groups and all the people living in rural areas will we be able to guarantee an active and attractive rural area for all. in short, i am in favour of a multifunctional rural area. i would particularly like to welcome the new leader+ programme for its global dimension. in the future, it may be possible that the programme will no longer apply to eligible areas only, but to all eu regions. this horizontal approach is entirely justified. this is how rural development programmes are drawn up. in future, funding will no longer be provided from three funds, but only from the eaggf guidance section. the quality of the programme will therefore need to be improved, as the eaggf must be an effective financial instrument. increased selection accuracy will lead to greater efficiency, as money must not be wasted thoughtlessly. i would also like to highlight a few points which constitute an approach which promotes an integrated development strategy which respects the environment. this area has a significant employment potential for the future of rural areas. we must use this potential if we are to present prospects to the rural world. a policy for rural areas is therefore not limited to a policy for farmers. leader+ must complement existing programmes, while avoiding overlaps and double funding. it must thus contribute to maximum overall development. in this way, leader+ and the other programmes under regulation 1257/99 can give greater prominence to the second pillar of the cap and have the most beneficial influence on rural areas as a whole.

- (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we have heard that by 2007 the number of jobs generated by tourism is expected to increase by 1.84 million. on the other hand, we know that in almost all developed countries there is a shortage of workers in the tourism sector, which cannot be fully filled even in times of high unemployment. this contradiction is understandable if one considers tourism as a two-faceted labour market. on the one hand, tourism creates employment in regions where supply is poor or even declining, in trade, industry and agriculture; mr president, i am shocked by the previous condemnation of austria by 14 member states when i now speak on the katiforis report. europe still lacks the optimism and conviction to be able to envisage a positive economic development. i think there are a number of reasons. in europe we have tax systems that are hindering performance. the average european income tax rate is over 50%. in the 1980s the reagan administration demonstrated, in a very courageous move, that a sharp reduction in taxes, from 70% to 28%, could permanently clean up the us budget. with this reduction, the tax yield on the highest incomes has not decreased, quite the contrary. compared with 1980, the tax return for 1984 fell from 17.6% to 25.6% for the highest incomes. we in europe should also have the courage to introduce a system of low taxation. but there are other areas which are playing a decisive role in our economic development, and which are not yet very positive. the legislative excesses in europe, the overly complicated laws have created a bureaucracy which means that in the end it takes 11 weeks to set up a company in the united states and a week and a half in the united kingdom. surveys of european universities show that 60% of students want to work in the public sector, and only 40% in the private sector. we need more competition in europe, especially in the field of new technologies, in order to guarantee the objectives of full employment and growth in the long term.

the rapporteur. - (de) madam president, ladies and gentlemen, if it is true that only what already has an internal unity, only what goes together, can be unified, then this raises the question of what european unity really is. we are not a people. we are not of the same ethnic origin. we don't speak the same language. we don't have a single culture or religion. our history is marked by war, by the wars we have fought against each other, and the uncertain borders of europe do not allow us to recognize ourselves clearly as a geographical entity. what is it that connects us? what makes us live together? the answer may seem fragile to some. but i think it represents everything we have: democracy and human rights. this is the unity of europe. despite all the suspicious things that this continent has produced, democracy and human rights are a message that is undeniably valid everywhere in the world. after a 20th century of horror and ultimate crime, this is the moral imperative of this continent. human dignity is intangible. that is why we cannot be satisfied with the fact that all the member states are parties to the european convention on human rights but the union is not. it is therefore only necessary that eu law should be in breach of national law, or even constitutional law, in the member states, without the union itself having a clear and comprehensive fundamental legal order. we should therefore be concerned that new legal conflicts will arise in the field of information and communication sciences and biotechnology, without europe being able to respond to them in the form of a legal consensus. we cannot therefore ignore the obvious weakening of parliamentary and legal control - and hence of the guarantee of fundamental rights - in a highly sensitive area of political union, for example police and judicial cooperation or foreign and security policy. the european union will therefore remain incomplete as long as the rule of law and the power of the institutions continue to grow, but not as long as the rights of citizens to defence and freedom, and the legal guarantees and protection they enjoy, continue to grow. the european parliament has a long and unbroken tradition of defending fundamental and human rights. the decisions of the cologne and tampere summits can therefore also be seen as the implementation of demands which parliament has been repeating for many years. these demands of parliament are in keeping with this tradition and are due to the nature of things, the nature of fundamental rights, which means that no rights can be promised without becoming rights for citizens. the legal binding nature and access to the courts are irrevocably linked to fundamental and human rights. the same applies to the indivisibility of fundamental rights. all the policies, institutions and bodies of the european union must be subject to this charter of fundamental rights. otherwise, it would be a promise of fundamental rights for citizens and inhabitants of the union, which would remain a mere proclamation. it would only appear to meet the expectations of the citizens. and the resolution before you, if it is adopted, takes up an old demand of parliament which has never been more important than it is today. this charter of fundamental rights must be the cornerstone, the essential element of the necessary process of giving the european union a constitution. the establishment of a european democracy in a supranational space is linked to this charter of fundamental rights. madam president, perhaps it is a quirk of history that you have today appointed an austrian as rapporteur, as co-rapporteur, for this charter of fundamental rights. i thank you for this confidence, and it may be unusual, but with your permission i dignity is intangible. - (de) mr president, i should like to take up where the rapporteur, whom i thank for the work he has done, left off and remind you once again that in the profusion of resolutions which do not correspond to the priorities of this house, the increase in foreign reserves is an important resolution. what is the legal basis for today's debate? according to article 3 of the statute of the european system of central banks, the ecb is responsible for the holding and management of the official monetary reserves. this task is carried out jointly by the ecb and the national central banks, which have transferred part of their reserves up to a maximum of eur 50 billion to the ecb, as provided for in the first paragraph of article 30 of the statute. article 30 (4) already stipulated at the time - today's discussion is therefore not surprising - that the ecb may require, in addition to the amount referred to in article 30 (1), the provision of other monetary reserves. this request must take the form of a regulation of the council of the eu. today, an additional payment of 50 billion euros is being requested. as the rapporteur has already said, the group of the european people's party (christian democrats) and the european democrats, as well as the whole of the committee on economic and monetary affairs, welcome this proposal. the reasons have been sufficiently explained and time is running out, but let me say that we believe the increase is necessary. first, because it contributes to strengthening the financial independence of the ecb, an essential condition for the stability and unassailable character of this currency. the yield on the funds paid up to now and on the official reserves is the ecb's sole source of income, with which it has to finance its own operations. we know that there were losses in 1999. we must therefore say that the lack of resources prevents the ecb from pursuing an independent and transparent policy. the need for financing must therefore be met so that this independence is never called into question. secondly, we are in favour of this increase because it contributes to the credibility of the ecb in the markets. the financial independence of the ecb, so that it can operate itself, will also strengthen its credibility on international markets and thus support the euro in the run-up to stage three, which will begin on 1 january 2002. thirdly, this increase will ensure the ecb's capacity to react and adapt. in the event of a serious imbalance in the markets, the european central bank will be able, by means of a possible doubling of its reserves, to carry out its tasks in the field of stability policy more quickly and more efficiently. it will have a more flexible capacity to react because it will have the means to do so. in our report we also introduced an additional model for enhancing the transparency of this process. i ask you to approve the increase in reserves. the rapporteur. - (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, first of all i would like to thank very warmly all those who supported me in the drafting of this report and, in particular, the members of the committee for adopting this report unanimously. in the last few weeks, we have seen daily, in all the media, the images of the terrible famine in ethiopia. as we enjoy our evening meal, the tv broadcasts distressing news of a people suffering from drought and famine. we voted this afternoon on a european parliament resolution on the famine in ethiopia precisely to respond to this situation. the european parliament, as one of the most important political institutions in the european union, has an obligation to speak out on this matter and it is important that we do not remain silent on such disasters and that we provide our political, moral and financial support, especially because the eu is one of the main donors. the international food aid convention, which we are discussing today in plenary, could not be placed in a more contemporary context and its importance and timeliness could not be better highlighted. this international convention should contribute to global food security and also provide the opportunity to respond to acute food crises and other food needs. in general terms, i would like to welcome the new convention because it seems to me to contain some very positive approaches and to widen the range of products concerned. let me make a few general remarks, however. firstly, and in any case, food must be guaranteed in the event of emergencies, droughts and other climatic disasters. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. better coordination between donor countries through a binding strategy will also contribute to the real effectiveness of aid. secondly, we should consider the long-term objective of our food policy, because we must ensure food security in all parts of the world and hence not have to resort to food aid in an emergency. the provision of food aid should be gradually replaced by measures to increase food security in the countries concerned. thirdly, it is extremely important in food policy to take account of its impact on local production and markets. these effects must be monitored and, as i said earlier, they cannot threaten local production because that would, in my view, be counterproductive. fourthly, the commission is invited to undertake an evaluation of food aid in order to ensure full consistency with other areas of development cooperation and the principles underlying them. the results must naturally influence future work; otherwise, it would be meaningless. fifthly, we must at all costs ensure that aid reaches the people who really need it. in this respect, i would strongly urge that we involve women in particular in the collaboration and planning of the implementation of these programmes because i think it would be a step in the right direction. sixthly, i would like to stress one more point, namely the problem of the delivery of food aid on the basis of credits. i therefore welcome the limitations introduced by the new convention because, in the long run, our aim must be to abolish the system of appropriations outright. we must do more to develop food aid in the form of donations. i would now like to say that i fully support the proposal put forward by mr fiori in the plenary. in conclusion, a word on the forthcoming wto negotiations. i would like to express my most serious reservations about a link between the liberalization of world trade and the delivery of humanitarian aid, because that would be at the expense of the hungry people and it cannot be in the interest of anyone.
mr president, the socialist group and i particularly welcome this agreement between the european community on the one hand and the swiss confederation on the other. it's a step in the right direction. switzerland is in the middle of europe, but it is not yet in the union. the conclusion of the bilateral negotiations with switzerland will create a new basis for the relationship between the union and switzerland and we all hope that in a few weeks the swiss people will give their approval to the present agreements. however, some areas still need clarification. i am not thinking about what occupies most of my thoughts in this matter, namely better cooperation in the fight against tax evasion to the detriment of european taxpayers. i should like to stick strictly to the subject of freedom of movement, and especially of frontier workers. in total there are 140 000 frontier workers in switzerland who come from germany, france, italy and austria. these workers are strongly affected by the recent deterioration of the swiss pension regulations. until the tenth revision of old-age and survivors' insurance, there was a so-called couple's pension, the calculation of which did not take account of the residence of the spouse. with the recent tenth revision, this rule has been removed. it has been replaced by allowances for educational work which is, however, linked to residence in switzerland. this means that crossborder workers in the european union must pay the same contributions as persons residing in switzerland but receive a pension which may be 30% lower. the eleventh revision, which is under way, even provides that widows' pensions can only be claimed by widows living in switzerland, which of course leads to a further deterioration in the conditions of eu frontier workers. unfortunately, the governments of the european union have also paid too little attention to this subject so far. i hope that will change in the near future. the current rules on old-age and survivors' insurance are contrary to the principle of equal treatment and fairness. the frontier workers pay the same amount of pension contributions as their swiss colleagues, but receive a considerably reduced pension. i hope that this unilateral discrimination by the swiss government will disappear as a result of this bilateral agreement. i am particularly grateful to the rapporteur for having raised and taken up this issue in the explanatory statement of his report. a map shows that these were probably not the last negotiations and agreements between the union and switzerland. negotiations and agreements which we will have to conduct and decide on bilaterally.

because an artist needs to be heard. whether the cd is a pirated copy or not is secondary to him, for once he is famous enough to have pirated copies of his cds, he can do without these royalties. but he's creating a market for himself to perform in. that's just a side detail. there is also a terribly bad rumor, which of course is not verifiable, that many of these imitations and piracies are happening under the guise of industry itself. this is the most reprehensible thing! the approach to be taken would be that we should - if this were really true, we should also check - not punish the consumers in the first place, but apply the penalties where production takes place. it is also a difference for me whether a small family in a poorer country in a so-called family business is trying to improve its life a little by imitating, or whether under the protective cloak of industry, said t-shirts or other products of our consumer world are simply sold there in order to create a market. i'd like to see that from a different angle. in conclusion, i would like to stress once again that i am sceptical as to whether the final consumer is really the one who should be punished so severely, because today it is hardly possible to distinguish between the original and the copy. it takes a trained eye!

the rapporteur. - (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the importance of the flax and hemp sector may not seem obvious at first sight, especially to our colleagues who are less concerned with agricultural matters. in europe, flax and hemp are traditional arable crops, but their popularity has declined in the meantime and their multiple uses are only being rediscovered today. that is why we must encourage at european level the possibilities of an agricultural sector to develop products for the future. insulating materials for houses, fillings for vehicle bodies, and clamping plates for furniture are just a few examples of modern technology. the manufacture of these materials from renewable raw materials must offer a real alternative to synthetic materials and therefore also become an attractive new source of income for farmers who supply raw materials. the need for reform is based primarily on the abuse and fraud we have seen in recent years in the area of bonuses. i categorically refuse this bounty hunt; the commission has therefore decided to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: however, the commission's proposals go a little further than that in my view. let me briefly outline the considerations which underlie my report. the area premium is to be fixed at 126 /t or 600 /ha and is to be paid only if proof is provided that the processor has been delivered. the area premium shall be determined in accordance with the regulation on the support scheme for arable crop producers; the commission has therefore decided to amend the regulation to take account of the new characteristics of flax and hemp. it is therefore clear that the plans for the regional base areas must be extended to cover flax and hemp-lined areas. since flax and hemp are mainly grown for non-food purposes and no surplus is produced, there should be no obligation to set aside the crops, with frozen areas serving as a market-regulating instrument in the event of surpluses. the processing premium, payable to the first processor, is only granted if proof of processing carried out within 18 months can be provided. the commission has also proposed that the maximum rate of the premium be fixed at € 120 per tonne for long fibres and € 70 per tonne for short fibres and hemp. in this respect, the premium must naturally be guaranteed in the long term not only for long fibres but also for short fibres; the short fibre is of crucial importance for innovative projects. the commissioner, the national maximum quantities, the eu maximum quantity proposed by the commission, must be reconsidered. the maximum quantities for member states should be set on the basis of existing processing capacities and adapted to the planned processing capacities for which irreversible investments have already been made. let me say a few words about hemp, to dispel the constant misunderstandings about it. the risk of drug culture does not exist in europe and the use of fenugreek for nutritional purposes is a well-established practice at regional level. the existing control mechanisms and the thresholds applied are, in my view, sufficient to prevent the cultivation of hemp for the production of psychotropic substances. specialties have been developed in the different european regions, such as hemp seed oil or hemp beer, and contribute to the enrichment of regional identity through a food chain. mr president, i would like to emphasize once again that the flax and hemp sector, in its versatility, is so essential to the future of european agriculture and to the future of farmers as suppliers of raw materials in sectors of the future that a necessary reform, let us admit, must not render the whole sector unprofitable. the committee on agriculture and rural development adopted this report by a very large majority. i would therefore like to thank very much all those who have supported me in the preparation of this report.

the rapporteur. - (de) mr president, allow me to begin by making a few remarks on the decision of the court of first instance of 2 june on the rights and duties of members of this house vis-à-vis olaf. i'm glad that's the decision. it establishes that we as elected representatives of the people are entitled to claim a special status and that in cases of doubt, the protection of our independence must take priority. in fact, that was my position from the beginning. that is why, in the negotiations with the commission and the council, we have subjected all the sensitive passages of the olaf regulation to the reservations of the protocol on privileges and immunities. that is why we proposed last autumn in the committee on budgetary control that the provisions relating to members should be separated from those relating to officials. unfortunately, the committee on constitutional affairs did not follow this proposal at the time. i said from the start that the offices of members of parliament were off-limits to olaf. (applause) i would like to thank the commission's legal service clearly saw things differently. he just got a lecture from the court. however, the court's decision puts us in an unfair position because only the plaintiffs can waive that decision. it is impossible that this situation will continue until a decision is taken on the merits of the case, perhaps in two years' time. i have therefore used rule 181 of the rules of procedure to propose an amendment to the rules of procedure. the text i am proposing follows the original recommendation of the committee on budgetary control. it shall strictly adhere to the interinstitutional agreement concluded with the commission and the council and shall take full account of the allegations of the court of first instance. i ask you to support this initiative to amend the rules of procedure. in this way we could clarify matters as quickly as possible and i think we could even create the conditions for colleagues to withdraw the complaint they have lodged with the court. if the proposal to amend the regulation is implemented, the complaint would be without purpose in any case. because one thing is also clear from the court's decision: the parliament's internal decision was not in order, but the regulation of the parliament and the council of 25 may 1999 on the action of olaf exists and is not called into question. i cannot therefore understand why some of the amendments to my report claim that the court's decision has strengthened the position of the european investment bank and the european central bank in their opposition to the regulation. the two banks claim that the regulation of the parliament and of the council does not apply to them in any way because it would undermine their independence. i do not understand this at all, because it is not a question of interfering in the work of the banks! the point is to guarantee a minimum degree of protection against fraud for them too. or do we prefer to leave this job to private detective agencies? the european court of justice and the court of auditors have properly implemented the provisions of the olaf regulation by means of internal decisions. they certainly would not have done so if they had perceived a threat to their independence. the two banks have not even the slightest plausible explanation for their refusal to accept what the independent judges sitting in the european court of justice have accepted. three points on the content of my report. element 1, olaf is a unit which investigates and deals with contentious cases quickly and efficiently. the legal conditions have been created. we have done everything we can with the council. the development of olaf is in full swing. the second element is the european financial prosecutor, who would initially be entitled to assist national courts in prosecuting offences committed by officials or other servants of the union. the third element is really effective disciplinary procedures. the statute and the financial regulation have long provided that in the event of gross negligence or intentional action, the responsible decision-makers must pay for the damage from their own pockets. this provision has never been applied. the commission is even now considering removing it from the financial regulation. but that would be the wrong signal to give. we call on the commission to reconsider such a move. the problem with these disciplinary procedures at the moment is that they inevitably lead to huge conflicts of interest. in the case of senior officials, the college of commissioners decides to open such a procedure and it is the commission itself which ultimately pronounces the judgment, on the proposal of a disciplinary council in which senior officials must debate the fate of senior officials and where people sit to judge the case of their colleagues. it cannot work and it does not work. as long as this system is maintained, there will be constant complaints, whether justified or not, that these procedures are being manipulated. the fact that in future the disciplinary councils will be interinstitutional - that is, staff from different institutions will be represented on them - will not change this. the fact that the commission is sticking to the proposal for genuine outsourcing, as already decided by this house on 19 january in the context of the report by our colleague mr van hulten, will not change that. in this resolution, parliament called for disciplinary proceedings in the case of infringements of the financial regulation to be entrusted to an external chamber close to the court of justice or the court of auditors so that there would be no doubt as to the objectivity and fairness of the procedure. we have made this clear in this report by providing for the creation of such a chamber in the court of auditors with the court of justice as the appellate body. as regards olaf, the report also takes up some points from the white paper on the reform of the commission. i have just heard one or the other say again that we should postpone the decision on these points and not take a position at the moment. however, the commission intends to implement all the points that are discussed before the summer break. if we do not take a stand now, we may well be sparing ourselves the opportunity to express our opinion. a final word to commissioner schreyer: from conversations last week with members of the olaf monitoring committee, i know that olaf's recruitment and appointment procedures are proceeding too slowly. this is probably also because the commission is clearly too inflexible, whereas on paper it has transferred full powers to the director of olaf by a proper decision. i can only urge you to respect your own decisions and not to make unnecessary trouble. - (de) my vote is to support the rapporteur's efforts to avoid the proposed reduction in the monthly cereals surcharges. the conclusions adopted by the european council at the berlin summit made it clear that the monthly increases should be maintained at their current value of eur 1 per tonne and frozen at that level for the entire period from 2000 to 2006. the proposed reduction in the monthly increases would therefore be a clear breach of the farmers' word. given the worrying economic situation of the rural population, a further loss of income cannot be justified. in real terms, the income of people employed in agriculture has fallen by almost 10% since 1996. a reduction in the monthly increases would further worsen the economic situation of farmers.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. - (de) mr president, i hope that the two speakers who were to come before me did not arrive by train and were not delayed. i believe they have arrived at parliament and will be on time. unfortunately, it is a fact that the railway has to face many problems inherited from the past. in 1970, for example, the railway market share in freight transport was 21%, whereas today it is only 8.5%. rail has suffered massive losses in freight transport but also in passenger transport, its share falling from 10 to 6%. the road haulage sector has won the battle and continues to grow and gain market share: in austria, for example, annual growth at the eastern border is 16%. since 1990, it has increased by 2.4 times. looking at the traffic of trucks and buses from eastern europe on the most important east-west axis, the a1 motorway, we see that traffic increased sixfold between 1995 and 2000. we can therefore see that the transport of goods, and in particular the transit of goods, is growing massively. it is estimated that the increase will exceed 100 percent between 1995 and 2015. that is why it is important and a priority for us to deal with the european rail network. this network has a total of 156,000 kilometers. the tens cover 78,000 km of land. by 2010, 12 600 km of new sections will be built for speeds above 250 km/h and 16 300 km of existing sections will be upgraded to allow speeds above 200 km/h. it is estimated that the total cost - the sum is truly enormous - will reach 186 billion euros by 2010. we are aware of the importance of investment for the european union. we are therefore called upon to set urgent priorities. i think the time has come to adapt the railway to market conditions so that the railway invests even more in the needs of its customers, to set priorities together, to establish an effective plan and a tight timetable. the essential requirements are, of course, the standardisation of the track gauge throughout europe, and particularly where more than two countries are working together. there are still five different track widths. we need a unique electrification, especially in terms of voltage and frequency. the technical characteristics of the supply current must be standardised so that electricity can be purchased from public networks or fed back into these public networks without significant losses. the third point, which i consider to be the most important, concerns the safety of trains. we need external control and certification systems. the operator of the railway infrastructure shall not be responsible for the verification of the infrastructure. it is also natural that independent certification bodies should be used to check cars and lorries. i believe that it is urgent, in view of the recent rail accidents, that external control bodies should ensure the safety of passengers in europe.

simplistic. in fact, there are four aspects to distinguish. first, there is prevention. it has hardly happened in the case of kosovo. secondly, there is the deterrent. in kosovo, it was probably too timid, because the common will was not perceptible. thirdly, the ability to intervene quickly and wisely. this may have worked in a relative way, although disagreement over the ideal mode of intervention has sometimes had a blocking effect. fourthly, peace must be restored and stabilized, especially after military intervention. the developments of the last days have shown that the task is not as simple as it may seem. i am therefore particularly grateful that the question of the police and security forces in kosovo has been raised on many occasions. it is absolutely essential to - (de) mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the motion for a resolution on the implementation of a common policy on european security and defence is an important signal from the european parliament before the feira summit; this signal goes both ways. firstly, it is a manifestation of the role which the european union wants to play in a completely independent way in terms of civil and military security policy, and this within a clearly defined framework, as stipulated in the petersberg peace-making and peacekeeping missions. secondly, it also sends a signal that the role of the european parliament must be respected and strengthened in defining the common european security and defence policy. the european union is lagging behind in terms of common security and defence, given that its defence spending is less than 60% of the comparable us expenditure, and that only 10% of the us operational efficiency can be achieved with it. the practical obligation to create a european armed force of up to 60 000 men to carry out these petersberg missions requires the solidarity of all the member states of the european union. the absence of a genuine common foreign and security policy was made more acute by the balkan crisis. it highlighted the weaknesses of the european union in this area. there can be no question of doubling nato's functions in the field of global security. nor can there be any question of creating a costly parallel structure. nevertheless, the petersberg missions must be guaranteed to be carried out. in addition, the principle that security is an indivisible whole must prevail. mutual assistance, as laid down in article 5 of the treaty on western european union, must therefore be a reality open to all in a revised treaty on european union. the idea of adding article 5 in the form of an additional protocol to a revised treaty to which member states may be able to accede individually is a clever start.

- (de) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the prohibition of all forms of television advertising for cigarettes and other tobacco products laid down in directive 89/552, and the extension introduced by that directive to the labelling obligation applicable to all tobacco products, are an expression of the ambivalent attitude of the european union towards tobacco. on the one hand, tobacco cultivation in the european union is subsidised annually to the tune of over one billion euros, while on the other hand, attempts are being made to reduce tobacco consumption in the union as much as possible. this conflict of objectives must be resolved in the interests of the credibility of community policy. the european union must set itself the objective of pursuing a policy that is understandable to the citizen. the promotion of tobacco cultivation on the one hand and the prohibition of tobacco advertising on the other are two contradictory issues. if this contradiction cannot be resolved at community level, then the european union must commit itself at international level to achieving a reduction in tobacco cultivation, in order to contribute to a high level of health protection.

the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. he means what he says. common principles have been more important than isolated political objectives. i would also like to thank my committee, the committee on legal affairs and the internal market, very quickly for the trust it has placed in a neo-politician by bringing this sensitive subject before it. the right of asylum is enshrined in various conventional arrangements; in this respect, definitions and interpretations vary from one national court to another, so that the right is not guaranteed uniformly. this is clearly contrary to the foundations and objectives of the ec treaty, whose title 4 cites asylum policy as an important measure to establish an area of freedom, security and justice. the creation of a uniform right of asylum and harmonised implementing provisions is therefore an objective of the community. the document presented by the commission explains first the need to adopt legally binding instruments on asylum procedures. eight specific points are addressed: the communitization of the dublin system, eurodac, minimum standards for reception, minimum conditions for the recognition of refugee status, a procedure for granting or withdrawing refugee status, complementary or subsidiary protection, temporary protection for displaced persons and - not the least important element - burden sharing. we have also succeeded in drawing up a consensus document in the legal affairs committee, and i am very pleased with that. here again, we did not put our different policy objectives in the foreground, but we did write a good opinion which was useful for mr schmitt's report. i think, however, that we shall have a fruitful vote tomorrow in favour of our common ideals. i hope that we all have the strength and courage to pursue the points you mentioned at the end of your speech.

this is essential. this is fundamental, because asylum seekers have the right to a swift decision and a swift response as regards their future status. the third point is that which confers justice and contributes to parallel decision-making in all the member states. this is our obligation to draw up - and keep up to date - lists of safe third countries and countries of origin. it would make our job a lot easier if we could compile these lists. a broad consensus has been reached, but i fear that this consensus cannot be maintained if the greens table all their amendments. they want many positive things, but sometimes they want to have it all, as mrs merkel once remarked. if they want to, for example, completely expand the concept of refugee or work towards full access for asylum seekers to the labour market or towards a reversal of the burden of proof, this again seriously threatens the consensus that has been reached with their approval in committee. i would urge them to consider withdrawing their amendments so that the broad consensus can be maintained.

the rapporteur. (de) mr president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, one might think that the three reports we are debating today are in fact technical reports. however, after presenting them to my group today, i have seen the emotions they can evoke. i am not saying that the commission should not be involved in this, but i am saying that it should be involved in the work of the european parliament. the question is how important they are in europe. what many opponents of reform forget is that at present and in previous years the share of rail transport has been steadily decreasing. trains are still running in europe, but fewer and fewer goods are being loaded onto their cars, and this is a state of affairs which we cannot accept. i therefore think - and this is a view which we all share because we want to develop a european rail network - that the council's decision, the council's consensus, is a kind of minimal consensus which does not satisfy us, even if we have divergent views on certain things and on how to go further than the council has done. it is also, of course, a matter of correcting many past neglects, since only national railway companies have been established so far. however, we are talking about globalisation. today, private companies are all active at transnational level; it is only in the railway sector that we have not really heard of it yet. so we are talking about liberalization and opening up the market and it goes without saying that there are several conceptions of this. some people see the market as this invisible hand, this quasi-divine principle that should guide and direct everything, but others think that we should only open the market with caution and only where it is necessary. i think - at least that is our position and mine too - that there is a middle way on which we must make progress but that must be clearly defined as to how to open up the market while of course giving the railway companies the possibility of gradually adapting to the new conditions. for us, competition is a means of increasing efficiency and we must therefore increase rail traffic, particularly through freight transport, the most sensitive area, so that the services of railway workers are not constantly being reduced. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. firstly, we are overwhelmingly in favour of the idea that there should be a competition authority in every country, a regulator of sorts. in doing so, there would be a service per country to which all railway companies, all interested parties, shippers, who knows, would turn in the event of a breach of openness and transparency of competition. there can therefore be no exceptional rules. we are building a european rail network; not one network for one country and another for another. the exceptional regulations should therefore be abandoned. this does not mean that everything must be prescribed in the smallest detail, but the margins of freedom we grant should be the same for all countries and should not vary from one country to another. secondly, there is a difference of opinion on the mandatory separation of infrastructure. we believe that it should be possible to act in a neutral manner in competition matters even with integrated firms, provided of course that the balance sheet and certain basic conditions are kept separate. our proposal, my proposal, is that the commission should report to us after a few years and inform parliament of any breaches of the principles of free and transparent competition: we could then take another decision. we believe that liberalization must certainly concern first and foremost the transnational transport of goods, but also, ultimately, the national transport. however, we believe that it is important and sufficient, at least for the time being, to make clear that crossborder passenger transport must also be subject to the opening up of the market. the question of whether passenger transport should be liberalized between stuttgart and munich should, in my view, be decided by the german or bavarian federal governments, and not necessarily at european level. in this case, we believe that the member states should retain a margin of discretion. an important issue is the pricing system. what should the concession prices for a capacity be? there are two principles. the first concerns only marginal costs, the costs of passing an extra train. the other party considers that in principle all costs of providing the line, railway line, etc. should be covered. we believe that if in the short term the marginal cost principle is appropriate to ensure that there is competition and that trains can run again, then in the long term the full cost would probably have to be taken into account, but in parallel with the corresponding principle in the road sector. without this, we would not be able to transport more goods by rail, for example, and this is also a problem faced by some railway companies, the german railways, to give an example. in the present situation, the principle of full costs means that many people cannot accept railway offers because they are simply too expensive. it would therefore also be necessary to make a gradual transition in this area and to have a parallel development between rail and road. the last point i would like to make is that a criticism we hear frequently is that of the future of the public service and its eventual decline. is there only the principle of the market, the principle of the private economy? that is why - and i welcome the support we have received - we have reintroduced a provision which was already provided for in the area of public services, because, unfortunately, the commission has not yet put forward any general proposal on the treatment of public services. in the future, a country will be able to say that in certain areas, at certain times, public services must be given priority. their priority position can therefore most certainly be established in this area. i therefore believe that the decision we take tomorrow will certainly trigger a conciliation procedure. it's not a particular problem. the two rapporteurs and all the groups in this house are ready to find a compromise. however, the council must also take a step forward. we have taken a step forward and if the council does the same, we can reach a compromise. in conclusion, i would like to thank all the members of my group and also other groups in parliament who have done a great deal of work - including the commission, of course - so that tomorrow we will have two or three very good reports.
- (de) mr president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, when you receive a large number of letters from people you do not know, there are two reasons for this. either you're very famous or the subject of the correspondence is very important. the european parliamentarians are not very famous: this must be the subject of this correspondence. in the last few days we have all received many letters, documents and requests for interviews, almost all from representatives of national railway companies. all of them, without exception, urged us not to proceed with the mandatory separation of the farm and the infrastructure, as requested by the proposals of the rapporteur, mr georg jarzembowski. mr swoboda also touched on this subject. however, he argued that liberalization should be introduced gradually. georg jarzembowski argues that our current systems of quasi-integrated state monopoly have not led to an optimal use of the systems. who could argue with that ? the numbers are very clear. our present railways are not really what we urgently need in europe, namely an efficient and accepted system. all the examples in related fields support the correctness of the argument. we're not going to be taking small steps anymore. the telecommunications sector has shown this, and so has electricity generation and many other areas. we also expect the same from the railways. they are primarily at the service of their users and thus at the service of a more efficient and functional european transport system. that is what we want, that is what we need and that is why we will vote for it.
